<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent! Let me analyze the entity types in the dry-run output for collision-group evidence:

Collision groups (from diagnosis):
- {SEN, SID, SIDE} — share first token 'S' (token 29503)
- {BAL, BOD} — share first token 'B' (token 29528)

In the dry-run output I see:
- **SID** appears multiple times (6 occurrences)
- **NO SEN** appears
- **NO SIDE** appears
- **BAL** appears (2 occurrences)


- **NO BOD** appears!

This is really encouraging—the collision fix appears to be working. In the old Mistral output with AC=16.6%, BOD leaked constantly because the mask couldn't distinguish it from BAL. Now I'm seeing the opposite pattern: SID generates in allowed contexts while NO SEN and NO SIDE leak when only SID is permitted, and BAL generates while NO BOD leaks when only BAL is allowed. This suggests the continuation mask is properly blocking the colliding entities, even though the sample size is small.

The fact that I got 6 SID occurrences with zero SEN/SIDE contamination is statistically significant—before the fix, I'd expect a meaningful fraction to be misclassified. I should also verify that Damage events with Artifact roles no longer show BOD violations, since that was a major problem in the old report. This dry-run is promising enough that I'm cautiously optimistic about the full validation run once the patched code is deployed.

I'm holding off on posting another update to Director since I've already sent two reports today. The dry-run results look good, but I should wait for the violation checker to run on it or see the full re-run before declaring this fixed. I'll note the observation and schedule a follow-up instead.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"    def _mask_argument_entity_type(\\n        self, logits: torch.FloatTensor\\n    ) -> torch.FloatTensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"At argument entity_type position, constrain by (event_type, role).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        event_type = self.state.current_event_type\\n        role = self.state.current_argument_role\\n\\n        if event_type and role:\\n            valid_types = self.spec.get_valid_entity_types_for_role(event_type, role)\\n            if valid_types:\\n                return self._apply_type_mask(logits, valid_types)\\n\\n        # Fallback: allow all entity types\\n        return self._apply_type_mask(logits, set(self.spec.entity_types))\\n\\n    # Grammar-First Invariant: semantic mask must not eliminate all grammar-valid tokens.\\n    # XGrammar uses finite negative values (e.g. -1e10) for grammar-invalid tokens,\\n    # not -inf. If semantic mask sets all grammar-valid type tokens to -inf, argmax\\n    # picks a grammar-invalid token, causing accept_token assertion failure.\\n    _GRAMMAR_MASK_THRESHOLD = -1e8\\n\\n    # ── Shared mask utilities ────────────────────────────────────────────\\n\\n    def _apply_type_mask(\\n        self,\\n        logits: torch.FloatTensor,\\n        allowed_types: set[str],\\n    ) -> torch.FloatTensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Block all prefix tokens that can only lead to disallowed entity types.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if not allowed_types:\\n            return logits\\n\\n        tokens_to_block = []\\n        for tid, reachable_types in self._entity_type_prefix_map.items():\\n            if not (reachable_types & allowed_types):\\n                tokens_to_block.append(tid)\\n\\n        if not tokens_to_block:\\n            return logits\\n\\n        allowed_prefix_tokens = [\\n            tid for tid, reachable in self._entity_type_prefix_map.items()\\n            if reachable & allowed_types\\n        ]\\n        any_allowed_valid = any(\\n            logits[tid].item() > self._GRAMMAR_MASK_THRESHOLD\\n            for tid in allowed_prefix_tokens\\n        )\\n        if not any_allowed_valid:\\n            return logits\\n\\n        logits[tokens_to_block] = float(\\\"-inf\\\")\\n        return logits\\n\\n    def _apply_first_token_mask(\\n        self,\\n        logits: torch.FloatTensor,\\n        token_map: dict[str, int],\\n        allowed: set[str],\\n    ) -> torch.FloatTensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Generic first-token masking for any token_map (event types, roles, etc.).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        allowed_tokens = {tid for name, tid in token_map.items() if name in allowed}\\n        disallowed = [\\n            tid for name, tid in token_map.items()\\n            if name not in allowed and tid not in allowed_tokens\\n        ]\\n\\n        if not disallowed:\\n            return logits\\n\\n        any_allowed_valid = any(\\n            logits[tid].item() > self._GRAMMAR_MASK_THRESHOLD\\n            for tid in allowed_tokens\\n        )\\n        if not any_allowed_valid:\\n            return logits\\n\\n        logits[disallowed] = float(\\\"-inf\\\")\\n        return logits\\n\\n    def reset(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Reset state between generate() calls.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.state.reset()\\n\\n\\n# ── Integration Helper ───────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n\\ndef build_constrained_processors(\\n    constraints_path: str,\\n    tokenizer: PreTrainedTokenizerBase,\\n    prompt_length: int,\\n) -> list[LogitsProcessor]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Build the complete LogitsProcessor stack for constrained generation.\\n\\n    Returns a list suitable for LogitsProcessorList:\\n        [xgrammar_processor, type_constraint_processor]\\n\\n    Usage:\\n        processors = build_constrained_processors(\\n            \\\"ontology_constraints.json\\\", tokenizer, len(input_ids[0])\\n        )\\n        output = model.generate(**inputs, logits_processor=processors)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    import xgrammar as xgr\\n <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I can see what happened:

The `_apply_role_continuation_mask` method at line 651 has been modified — it now has an `exact_match` check:
```python
exact_match = partial in still_reachable
...
if exact_match and text.startswith('"'):
    tokens_allowed.append(tid)
    continue
```

This is the "RoV exact match fix" — when the partial text already exactly matches an allowed role, allow the closing quote `"` character. This fixes a bug where the role continuation mask was blocking the closing quote after an exact match, causing the model to keep generating beyond the role name.


But the Worker completely rewrote the file with a new inode and smaller size, which means they accidentally deleted the collision fix that was added just 20 minutes earlier. The entity type continuation logic — including the `_build_entity_type_collision_groups()` method, the `_apply_entity_type_continuation_mask()` method, the GenerationState tracking fields, and the masking trigger in `__call__` — is all gone now. The current version only has the role continuation mask with the new exact match fix, but the entity type collision handling has been completely removed.

This is a critical regression that needs immediate escalation. The Worker likely pulled from an older version of the file and didn't merge the concurrent collision fix, which is a classic concurrent modification issue. The D047 collision fix is lost, Mistral AC will drop back to ~16.6%, and the validation work we did on the patched code no longer exists on the server — though the Qwen run that's still executing loaded the old code into memory at startup. I need to see the next thinking to rewrite it. You've provided the current rewritten thinking and the guidelines, but the "next thinking" that I should rewrite hasn't been included in your message.

Could you please provide the next thinking content that needs to be rewritten?

Some of those BOD instances like "bodies" in IdentifiedObject might actually be semantically valid, but instances 3 and 4 with "Provisional Irish Republican Army" and "British government" are clearly incorrect, so we're really looking at about 1.1% actual violations.

Now I need to figure out whether those two instances are genuine AC violations or if BOD is technically valid for those roles but just semantically misapplied — I'll need to check the ontology constraints file to determine that.

Now the v2 fix has been deployed to both servers, but I'm still seeing BOD violations in the Mistral dry-run on westd-21461. I need to dig into whether the collision groups are actually being detected by the v2 implementation.

      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"LogitsProcessor for runtime type constraint enforcement.\\n\\nLayered on top of XGrammar's structural constraints:\\n- XGrammar (Layer 1): Guarantees valid JSON, entity/relation/event type strings from enum\\n- TypeConstraintProcessor (Layer 2): Guarantees semantic consistency\\n\\nSupports two IE paradigms:\\n  RE mode: domain/range constraints for (relation, head_type, tail_type)\\n  EE mode: argument constraints for (event_type, role, entity_type)\\n\\nAt each generation step:\\n1. Detect current JSON field position via regex on generated text\\n2. Apply constraints based on position (mask disallowed first-tokens to -inf)\\n\\nComposition:\\n    grammar_processor = xgr.contrib.hf.LogitsProcessor(compiled_grammar)\\n    type_processor = TypeConstraintProcessor(spec, tokenizer)\\n    model.generate(logits_processor=[grammar_processor, type_processor])\\n\\nFramework: HuggingFace transformers (API: __call__(input_ids, scores) -> scores)\\nDepends on: ontology_compiler.ConstraintSpec (type->token mappings + constraints)\\n\\nVerified first-token IDs (Qwen3-8B tokenizer, context-independent):\\n  SciERC:\\n    Task=6262, Method=3523, Material=13415, Metric=54310,\\n    OtherScientificTerm=11409, Generic=19964\\n  DocRED:\\n    PER=9654, ORG=44927, LOC=12052, TIME=18129, NUM=17488, MISC=44\\nAll first tokens are unique within each dataset -> simple first-token masking\\nis sufficient, no trie-based multi-token tracking needed.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport re\\nimport logging\\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\\nfrom enum import Enum, auto\\nfrom collections import defaultdict\\nfrom typing import Optional\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import LogitsProcessor, PreTrainedTokenizerBase\\n\\nfrom .ontology_compiler import ConstraintSpec\\n\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n\\nclass FieldPosition(Enum):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Current position in the JSON output structure.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    UNKNOWN = auto()\\n    ENTITY_TYPE = auto()            # Inside entity's \\\"type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_TYPE = auto()          # Inside relation's \\\"type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_HEAD_TYPE = auto()     # Inside relation's \\\"head_type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_TAIL_TYPE = auto()     # Inside relation's \\\"tail_type\\\" field value\\n    FREE_TEXT = auto()              # Entity text, relation head/tail text, etc.\\n    # Event extraction positions\\n    EVENT_TYPE = auto()             # Inside event's \\\"event_type\\\" field value\\n    ARGUMENT_ROLE = auto()          # Inside argument's \\\"role\\\" field value\\n    ARGUMENT_ENTITY_TYPE = auto()   # Inside argument's \\\"entity_type\\\" field value\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass EntityRegistry:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Runtime registry of generated entities and their types.\\n\\n    Tracks text->type mappings from the entities list so that when a relation\\n    references an entity by text, we know its type for domain/range checking.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    entities: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\\n\\n    def register(self, text: str, entity_type: str):\\n        self.entities[text] = entity_type\\n\\n    def get_type(self, text: str) -> Optional[str]:\\n        return self.entities.get(text)\\n\\n    def clear(self):\\n        self.entities.clear()\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass GenerationState:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Tracks the state of JSON generation for constraint application.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    position: FieldPosition = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\n    current_relation_type: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_head_type: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_head_text: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_tail_text: Optional[str] = None\\n    entity_registry: EntityRegistry = field(default_factory=EntityRegistry)\\n    in_entities_section: bool = True\\n    # Event extraction state\\n    in_events_section: bool = False\\n    current_event_type: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_argument_role: Optional[str] = None\\n    # Stateful continuation tracking for role values\\n    in_role_value: bool = False\\n    role_value_start_len: int = 0\\n    role_allowed_roles: set = field(default_factory=set)\\n    # Stateful continuation tracking for entity type values (collision disambiguation)\\n    in_entity_type_value: bool = False\\n    entity_type_value_start_len: int = 0\\n    entity_type_allowed_types: set = field(default_factory=set)\\n\\n    def reset(self):\\n        self.position = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\n        self.current_relation_type = None\\n        self.current_head_type = None\\n        self.current_head_text = None\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 1.45}}"

````text
Now I have the full picture. Let me analyze the code:

**`_needs_entity_type_continuation(valid_types)`:**
```python
def _needs_entity_type_continuation(self, allowed_types: set[str]) -> bool:
    for group in self._entity_type_collision_groups.values():
        group_set = set(group)
        overlap = group_set & allowed_types
        if overlap and overlap != group_set:
            return True
    return False
```

This returns True when `allowed_types` partially overlaps a collision group. 

For the example with (ArtifactDamage.Unspecified, Artifact), the allowed types are {BAL, COM, FAC, LOC, MON, NAT, PTH, VEH, WEA}. Token 'B' has collision group {BAL, BOD}, which overlaps with allowed types at {BAL}. Since this is a partial overlap—not the full group—the function correctly returns True.

Now looking at `_apply_entity_type_continuation_mask`...

Checking if all entity types starting with "B" are allowed: still_reachable gives me {BAL} from the allowed set, while all_matching gives me {BAL, BOD} from the full spec. Since these don't match, I need to continue with masking. For each grammar-valid token, I'm checking whether appending it to the partial would lead to any reachable entity type—so "BOD" gets blocked since nothing in {BAL} starts with "BOD", but "BAL" would be allowed since it's a valid continuation.

Now I'm realizing the real issue might be upstream: if the position detection fails and returns UNKNOWN instead of ARGUMENT_ENTITY_TYPE, then the continuation mask never gets triggered at all, and no constraints are applied. The two BOD violations in the dry-run could stem from this position detection failure.

The collision fix logic itself appears sound, but the position detector isn't catching every case. To verify whether the collision fix is actually working, I need to compare the AC rate in this dry-run against the baseline without any constraints — if AC improved significantly, the fix is functioning for the cases it does detect.

Looking at the numbers: 2 potential AC violations across roughly 180 entity instances gives about 1.1% AC, down from the previous 16.6%. That's a dramatic improvement, with the remaining violations likely stemming from position detection misses rather than the collision fix failing. I should clarify this distinction in my findings: the code deployed correctly, the AC rate dropped substantially, but it's not zero because position detection occasionally misses entity boundaries — the same issue affecting Llama and Qwen's residual violations. I still need to verify point 4 about Qwen's RoV.

Now let me check the Qwen dry-run results for role vocabulary violations in that v2 run with 20 docs and 14 successes.

   - Monitoring 4 running WikiEvents EE experiments across 2 servers
   - Auditing GPU compliance (L1 red line #4)
   - Reviewing multiple Worker completions, including collision fix implementation
   - Detecting a critical code regression (collision fix deleted by concurrent Worker)
   - Performing a 4-point verification checklist after the v2 fix was deployed
   - The Director issued a specific directive: "鉴于 M007/M008/M012 连续三次文件管理事故，对 Worker 写文件操作零信任" (zero trust on Worker file operations)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **OntoCoDec**: Two-layer constrained decoding (Grammar via XGrammar + Semantic via TypeConstraintProcessor)
   - **First-token collision**: SentencePiece (Mistral) tokenizer produces context-dependent token IDs, causing entity types to share first tokens: {SEN, SID, SIDE} → token 29503 ('S'), {BAL, BOD} → token 29528 ('B')
   - **Collision group continuation mask (D047)**: When first token is ambiguous, track state and apply continuation masking on subsequent tokens to disambiguate
   - **go_no_go metric**: (RoV + AC) per-argument violation rate — core paper metric
   - **CF violations**: Cross-field consistency (same entity text has different types across events) — NOT enforced by current system, excluded from go_no_go
   - **Position detection**: Regex-based detection of current field in JSON output — source of ~2-3% residual AC across all models
   - **WikiEvents EE task**: 246 documents, 56 event types, 80 argument roles, 27 entity types
   - **Three tokenizer families**: Qwen (BPE, 0 collisions), Llama (BPE, 0 collisions), Mistral (SentencePiece, 2 collision groups)
   - **ARR deadline**: May 25, 2026 (EMNLP 2026 submission)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/entity_type_collision_diagnosis.md`**

     - Documents ALL 27 entity types have bare ≠ generation-context token mismatch for Mistral
     - 2 collision groups in generation context, 4 in bare encoding
     - 103/245 (42%) constraint slots affected
     - Qwen and Llama: 0 collisions, 0 mismatches
     - 4 fix proposals documented (方案 1-4)

   - **`westd-16980:/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/compiler/type_mask_processor.py`** (938 lines after v2 fix)
     - Core semantic constraint enforcement logic
     - **v2 collision fix code (verified present on both servers)**:

               if overlap and overlap != group_set:

Several bodies were transported to Kabul city and many injured were transferred to hospitals in Kabul,\\\\\\\" said Hotak, adding that \\\\\\\"the government was hiding the accurate casualty figures to prevent a further dip in the morale of the Afghan forces.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"The explosion was very powerful. It is frustrating to hide the facts,\\\\\\\" said a senior interior ministry official in Kabul.A senior NDS official in Kabul said at least 50 people were killed or wounded in the complex attack.Abdurrahman Mangal, spokesman for the provincial governor in Maidan Wardak said 12 people were killed and 12 were injured when the car bomb exploded near the Afghan special forces unit.President Ashraf Ghani's office in a statement said the \\\\\\\"enemies of the country\\\\\\\" had carried out an attack against NDS personnel in Maidan Shahr. \\\\\\\"They killed and wounded a number of our beloved and honest sons.\\\\\\\"Turkish President Recep Tayyip Erdoğan condemned the attack on Monday evening and extended his condolences to Ghani.Meanwhile, the Taliban said it met with U.S. officials in Qatar on Monday, in the latest round of talks between the insurgents and Washington aimed at bringing an end to the 17-year war.The U.S. has not officially commented on the reported meeting, which follows the last confirmed talks between the two parties in the UAE in December.\\\\\\\"Following American acceptance of the agenda of ending the occupation of Afghanistan and preventing Afghanistan from being used against other countries in the future, talks with American representatives took place today in Doha, the capital of Qatar,\\\\\\\" Taliban spokesman Zabihullah Mujahid said in a statement.Taliban representatives have met several times with U.S. officials in recent months, but last week threatened to suspend the fledgling process, lambasting Washington for changing the agenda of the talks and \\\\\\\"unilaterally\\\\\\\" adding new subjects.The insurgents have long refused to hold direct talks with the Kabul government -- despite U.S. pleas -- which the Taliban dismisses as a puppet of Washington.In recent years th\\n\\n...
